What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I’ve been making a lot of careless mistakes lately and really struggle to pay attention to details. It’s been happening for about 11 months now, and\n\nDoctor: Did these attention problems start after a specific stressful life event, or have you had similar difficulties since childhood? Have they been present in more than one setting, like both work or school and at home?\n\nPatient: No, it didn't start from a stressful event; I've actually had these same struggles since childhood, definitely before I was 12 years old. They have just become much more noticeable over the last 11 months. It definitely affects me in multiple places, both with managing things at home and keeping up at work.\n\nDoctor: Besides making careless mistakes, do you often have trouble sustaining attention, seem not to listen when spoken to, or have difficulty organizing tasks and finishing them?\n\nPatient: I don't really relate to not listening when people talk to me or having general trouble sustaining attention. However, organizing my tasks and activities is definitely a major struggle for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often avoid or put off tasks that require a lot of mental effort, lose things you need, get easily distracted, or forget daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I struggle with those specific issues. I haven't really noticed myself losing things or avoiding tasks that require mental effort, and I don't think I'm easily distracted or forgetful with my daily routine.\n\nDoctor: How much are these attention and organization problems interfering with your work, home responsibilities, or relationships?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I haven't really thought about it in those terms, so I'm not quite sure how to measure the impact. I just know it's frustrating, but I haven't really stopped to analyze how much it is interfering with my work or relationships.\n\nDoctor: Can you give one or two specific examples of how these problems have affected your work or home life, such as missed deadlines, repeated mistakes, or trouble managing daily tasks?\n\nPatient: At work, I often submit major reports with glaring typos or completely overlook small but important data points because I miss the details. When it comes to organizing, I really struggle to plan out my daily tasks at home, so I end up starting three different chores at once and finishing none of them.\n\nDoctor: Do you also tend to feel unusually restless or fidgety, have trouble sitting still, talk excessively, or act impulsively, or are your difficulties mainly with attention and organization?\n\nPatient: Actually, I struggle with both aspects. I frequently fidget by tapping my hands or feet and often feel \"on the go\" as if driven by a motor. This is in addition to my major difficulties with attention and organization, like making careless mistakes and struggling to organize my tasks.\n\nDoctor: Besides fidgeting and feeling constantly on the go, do you often have trouble staying seated, talk excessively, blurt things out, have trouble waiting your turn, or interrupt others?\n\nPatient: I don't really experience those issues, like talking excessively, blurting things out, or interrupting others. Instead, my restlessness is much more physical, like constantly needing to fidget with my hands and feet or feeling like I am driven by a motor.\n\nDoctor: Have the fidgeting and feeling constantly on the go also been present since childhood and in more than one setting, like both at school or work and at home?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I’ve been fidgeting and feeling like I'm driven by a motor since I was a kid, well before I turned 12. It definitely happens in both places, whether I'm sitting at my desk at work or trying to wind down at home."

Instead, my restlessness is much more physical, like constantly needing to fidget with my hands and feet or feeling like I am driven by a motor.\n\nDoctor: Have the fidgeting and feeling constantly on the go also been present since childhood and in more than one setting, like both at school or work and at home?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely. I’ve been fidgeting and feeling like I'm driven by a motor since I was a kid, well before I turned 12.
